<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-subheader" style="font-size: 100%;"><div style="background-color: #E7AF97;"><a href="/wiki/IUCN_protected_area_categories" title="IUCN protected area categories">IUCN category</a> Ia (<a href="/wiki/Strict_nature_reserve" title="Strict nature reserve">strict nature reserve</a>)</div></td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Bouvet Island</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/uː/: &#39;oo&#39; in &#39;goose&#39;">uː</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span><span title="/eɪ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;face&#39;">eɪ</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">BOO</span>-vay</i></a>; <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_language" title="Norwegian language">Norwegian</a>: <i lang="no">Bouvetøya</i><sup id="cite_ref-polar-stadnamn-Bouvetøya_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-polar-stadnamn-Bouvetøya-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="no-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Norwegian" title="Help:IPA/Norwegian">[bʉˈvèːœʏɑ]</a></span>)<sup id="cite_ref-Berulfsen-1969_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Berulfsen-1969-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> is an island and dependency of <a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a>, and declared an uninhabited protected nature reserve. It is a <a href="/wiki/Subantarctic" title="Subantarctic">subantarctic</a> volcanic island, situated in the South <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">Atlantic Ocean</a> at the southern end of the <a href="/wiki/Mid-Atlantic_Ridge" title="Mid-Atlantic Ridge">Mid-Atlantic Ridge</a>, making it the world's most <a href="/wiki/Extreme_points_of_Earth#Remoteness" class="mw-redirect" title="Extreme points of Earth">remote</a> island. It is not part of the southern region covered by the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_Treaty_System" title="Antarctic Treaty System">Antarctic Treaty System</a>.
</p><p>The island lies 1,700&#160;km (1,100&#160;mi) north of the <a href="/wiki/Princess_Astrid_Coast" title="Princess Astrid Coast">Princess Astrid Coast</a> of <a href="/wiki/Queen_Maud_Land" title="Queen Maud Land">Queen Maud Land</a>, <a href="/wiki/Antarctica" title="Antarctica">Antarctica</a>, 1,900&#160;km (1,200&#160;mi) east of the <a href="/wiki/South_Sandwich_Islands" title="South Sandwich Islands">South Sandwich Islands</a>, 1,845&#160;km (1,146&#160;mi) south of <a href="/wiki/Gough_Island" title="Gough Island">Gough Island</a>, and 2,600&#160;km (1,600&#160;mi) south-southwest of the coast of South Africa. It has an area of 49&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (19&#160;sq&#160;mi), 93&#160;percent of which is covered by a <a href="/wiki/Glacier" title="Glacier">glacier</a>. The centre of the island is the ice-filled crater of an inactive <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcano</a>. Some <a href="/wiki/Skerry" title="Skerry">skerries</a> and one smaller island, <a href="/wiki/Lars%C3%B8ya" title="Larsøya">Larsøya</a>, lie along its coast. Nyrøysa, created by a rock slide in the late 1950s, is the only easy place to land and is the location of a <a href="/wiki/Weather_station" title="Weather station">weather station</a>.
</p><p>The island was first spotted on 1&#160;January 1739 by the Frenchman <a href="/wiki/Jean-Baptiste_Charles_Bouvet_de_Lozier" title="Jean-Baptiste Charles Bouvet de Lozier">Jean-Baptiste Charles Bouvet de Lozier</a>, during a French exploration mission in the <a href="/wiki/South_Atlantic" class="mw-redirect" title="South Atlantic">South Atlantic</a> with the ships <i>Aigle</i> and <i>Marie</i>. They did not make landfall. He mislabeled the coordinates for the island, and it was not sighted again until 1808, when the British <a href="/wiki/Whaler" title="Whaler">whaler</a> James Lindsay encountered it and named it <b>Lindsay Island</b>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mills-2003-1-96_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mills-2003-1-96-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The first claim to have landed on the island was made by the American sailor <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Morrell" title="Benjamin Morrell">Benjamin Morrell</a>, although this claim is disputed. In 1825, the island was claimed for the <a href="/wiki/British_Crown" class="mw-redirect" title="British Crown">British Crown</a> by George Norris, who named it <b>Liverpool Island</b>. He also reported having sighted another island nearby, which he named <a href="/wiki/Thompson_Island_(South_Atlantic)" title="Thompson Island (South Atlantic)">Thompson Island</a>, but this was later shown to be a <a href="/wiki/Phantom_island" title="Phantom island">phantom island</a>.
</p><p>In 1927, the <a href="/wiki/Hjalmar_Riiser-Larsen#The_Norvegia_expeditions" title="Hjalmar Riiser-Larsen">first <i>Norvegia</i> expedition</a> landed on the island, and claimed it for Norway. At that point, the island was given its current name of <i>Bouvet Island</i> ("Bouvetøya" in Norwegian).<sup id="cite_ref-wordpress-abandoned-lifeboat_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wordpress-abandoned-lifeboat-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> In 1930, following resolution of a dispute with the United Kingdom over claiming rights, it was declared a Norwegian dependency. In 1971, it was designated a <a href="/wiki/Nature_reserve" title="Nature reserve">nature reserve</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Discovery_and_early_sightings">Discovery and early sightings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Discovery and early sightings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_island_0.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Bouvet_island_0.jpg/220px-Bouvet_island_0.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Bouvet_island_0.jpg/330px-Bouvet_island_0.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Bouvet_island_0.jpg/440px-Bouvet_island_0.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1608" data-file-height="1070" /></a><figcaption>Southeast coast of Bouvet Island in 1898</figcaption></figure>
<p>The island was discovered on 1 January 1739 by <a href="/wiki/Jean-Baptiste_Charles_Bouvet_de_Lozier" title="Jean-Baptiste Charles Bouvet de Lozier">Jean-Baptiste Charles Bouvet de Lozier</a>, commander of the French ships <i>Aigle</i> and <i>Marie</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mills-2003-1-96_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mills-2003-1-96-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Bouvet, who was searching for a presumed large southern continent, spotted the island through the fog and named the cape he saw <a href="/wiki/Cap_de_la_Circoncision" class="mw-redirect" title="Cap de la Circoncision">Cap de la Circoncision</a>. He was not able to land and did not <a href="/wiki/Circumnavigate" class="mw-redirect" title="Circumnavigate">circumnavigate</a> his discovery, thus not clarifying if it was an island or part of <a href="/wiki/Terra_Australis" title="Terra Australis">a continent</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 62">&#58;&#8202;62&#8202;</span></sup> His plotting of its position was inaccurate,<sup id="cite_ref-Mill-1905_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mill-1905-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 47">&#58;&#8202;47&#8202;</span></sup> leading several expeditions to fail to find the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 58">&#58;&#8202;58&#8202;</span></sup> <a href="/wiki/James_Cook" title="James Cook">James Cook</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Second_voyage_of_James_Cook" title="Second voyage of James Cook">second voyage</a> set off from <a href="/wiki/Cape_Verde" title="Cape Verde">Cape Verde</a> on 22 November 1772 and attempted to find the island, but also failed.<sup id="cite_ref-Hough-1994_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hough-1994-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The next expedition to spot the island was in 1808 by James Lindsay, captain of the <a href="/wiki/Samuel_Enderby_%26_Sons" title="Samuel Enderby &amp; Sons">Samuel Enderby &amp; Sons</a>' (SE&amp;S) <a href="/wiki/Snow_(ship)" title="Snow (ship)">snow</a> <a href="/wiki/Whaler" title="Whaler">whaler</a> <i>Swan</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Burney-1817_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Burney-1817-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> <i>Swan</i> and another Enderby whaler, <a href="/wiki/Otter_(1807_ship)" title="Otter (1807 ship)"><i>Otter</i></a> were in company when they reached the island and recorded its position, though they were unable to land.<sup id="cite_ref-McGonigal-2003_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-McGonigal-2003-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Mills-2003_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mills-2003-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 434–435">&#58;&#8202;434–435&#8202;</span></sup> Lindsay could confirm that the "cape" was indeed an island.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 62">&#58;&#8202;62&#8202;</span></sup> The next expedition to arrive at the island was American <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Morrell" title="Benjamin Morrell">Benjamin Morrell</a> and his <a href="/wiki/Seal_hunting" title="Seal hunting">seal hunting</a> ship <i>Wasp</i>. Morrell, by his own account, found the island without difficulty (with "improbable ease", in the words of historian William Mills)<sup id="cite_ref-Mills-2003_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mills-2003-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 434–435">&#58;&#8202;434–435&#8202;</span></sup> before landing and hunting 196&#160;seals.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 62">&#58;&#8202;62&#8202;</span></sup> In his subsequent lengthy description, Morrell does not mention the island's most obvious physical feature: Its permanent ice cover.<sup id="cite_ref-Mill-1905_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mill-1905-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 106–107">&#58;&#8202;106–107&#8202;</span></sup> This has caused some commentators to doubt whether he actually visited the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Mills-2003_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mills-2003-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 434–435">&#58;&#8202;434–435&#8202;</span></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Simpson-Housley-1992_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Simpson-Housley-1992-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 10&#160;December 1825, SE&amp;S's George Norris, master of the <i>Sprightly</i>, landed on the island,<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 62">&#58;&#8202;62&#8202;</span></sup> named it Liverpool Island and claimed it for the <a href="/wiki/British_Crown" class="mw-redirect" title="British Crown">British Crown</a> and <a href="/wiki/George_IV" title="George IV">George IV</a> on 16&#160;December.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 63">&#58;&#8202;63&#8202;</span></sup> The next expedition to spot the island was Joseph Fuller and his ship <i>Francis Allyn</i> in 1893, but he was not able to land on the island. German <a href="/wiki/Carl_Chun" title="Carl Chun">Carl Chun</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Valdivia_Expedition" title="Valdivia Expedition">Valdivia Expedition</a> arrived at the island in 1898. They were not able to land, but dredged the seabed for geological samples.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> They were also the first to accurately fix the island's position.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 63">&#58;&#8202;63&#8202;</span></sup> At least three sealing vessels visited the island between 1822–1895. A voyage of exploration in 1927–1928 also took seal pelts.<sup id="cite_ref-Headland-2018_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Headland-2018-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Norris also spotted a second island in 1825, which he named <a href="/wiki/Thompson_Island_(South_Atlantic)" title="Thompson Island (South Atlantic)">Thompson Island</a>, which he placed 72&#160;km (45&#160;mi) north-northeast of Liverpool Island. Thompson Island was also reported in 1893 by Fuller, but in 1898 Chun did not report seeing such an island, nor has anyone since.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> However, Thompson Island continued to appear on maps as late as 1943.<sup id="cite_ref-ARH-NAM-1943_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARH-NAM-1943-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> A 1967 paper suggested that the island might have disappeared in an undetected volcanic eruption, but in 1997 it was discovered that the ocean is more than 2,400&#160;m (7,900&#160;ft) deep in the area.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Norwegian_annexation">Norwegian annexation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Norwegian annexation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg/220px-Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg/330px-Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg/440px-Bouvet_Island_1927.jpg 2x" data-file-width="796" data-file-height="521" /></a><figcaption>The annexation of the island on 1&#160;December 1927</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg/220px-Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="128" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg/330px-Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg/440px-Cape_Circoncision_-_Bouvet_Island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="465" /></a><figcaption>The first hut, built on <a href="/wiki/Kapp_Circoncision" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapp Circoncision">Kapp Circoncision</a>, in 1929</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1927, the First <i>Norvegia</i> Expedition, led by Harald Horntvedt and financed by <a href="/wiki/Lars_Christensen" title="Lars Christensen">Lars Christensen</a>, was the first to make an extended stay on the island. Observations and surveying were conducted on the island and <a href="/wiki/Oceanographic" class="mw-redirect" title="Oceanographic">oceanographic</a> measurements performed in the sea around it. At Ny Sandefjord, a small hut was erected and, on 1&#160;December, the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Norway" title="Flag of Norway">Norwegian flag</a> was hoisted and the island claimed for Norway. The annexation was established by a royal decree on 23&#160;January 1928.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 63">&#58;&#8202;63&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>The claim was initially protested by the United Kingdom, on the basis of Norris's landing and annexation. However, the British position was weakened by Norris's sighting of two islands and the uncertainty as to whether he had been on Thompson or Liverpool (i.e. Bouvet) Island. Norris's positioning deviating from the correct location combined with the island's small size and lack of a <a href="/wiki/Natural_harbour" class="mw-redirect" title="Natural harbour">natural harbour</a> made the UK accept the Norwegian claim.<sup id="cite_ref-Kyvik-2008_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kyvik-2008-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 52">&#58;&#8202;52&#8202;</span></sup> This resulted in diplomatic negotiations between the two countries, and in November 1929, Britain renounced its claim to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 63">&#58;&#8202;63&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Hjalmar_Riiser-Larsen#The_Norvegia_expeditions" title="Hjalmar Riiser-Larsen">Second <i>Norvegia</i> Expedition</a> arrived in 1928 with the intent of establishing a staffed meteorological radio station, but a suitable location could not be found.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 63">&#58;&#8202;63&#8202;</span></sup> By then both the flagpole and hut from the previous year had been washed away. The <a href="/wiki/Hjalmar_Riiser-Larsen#The_Norvegia_expeditions" title="Hjalmar Riiser-Larsen">Third <i>Norvegia</i> Expedition</a>, led by <a href="/wiki/Hjalmar_Riiser-Larsen" title="Hjalmar Riiser-Larsen">Hjalmar Riiser-Larsen</a>, arrived the following year and built a new hut at <a href="/wiki/Kapp_Circoncision" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapp Circoncision">Kapp Circoncision</a> and on Larsøya. The expedition carried out <a href="/wiki/Aerial_photography" title="Aerial photography">aerial photography</a> of the island and was the first Antarctic expedition to use aircraft.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 64">&#58;&#8202;64&#8202;</span></sup> The <i>Dependency Act</i>, passed by the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Norway" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament of Norway">Parliament of Norway</a> on 27&#160;February 1930, established Bouvet Island as a dependency, along with <a href="/wiki/Peter_I_Island" title="Peter I Island">Peter I Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Queen_Maud_Land" title="Queen Maud Land">Queen Maud Land</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-LOV19300227_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LOV19300227-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Eared_seal" title="Eared seal">eared seal</a> was protected on and around the island in 1929 and in 1935 all seals around the island were protected.<sup id="cite_ref-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Recent_history">Recent history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Recent history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1955, the South African frigate <a href="/wiki/SAS_Transvaal" title="SAS Transvaal">SAS <i>Transvaal</i></a> visited the island.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Nyrøysa, a rock-strewn ice-free area, the largest such on Bouvet, was created sometime between 1955 and 1958, probably by a landslide.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> In 1964, the island was visited by the British naval ship <a href="/wiki/HMS_Protector_(A146)" title="HMS Protector (A146)">HMS&#160;<i>Protector</i></a>. One of <i>Protector'</i>s two <a href="/wiki/Westland_Whirlwind_(helicopter)" title="Westland Whirlwind (helicopter)">Westland Whirlwind</a> helicopters landed a small survey team on the island led by <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_Commander" class="mw-redirect" title="Lieutenant Commander">Lieutenant Commander</a> Alan Crawford at Nyrøysa for a brief visit. Shortly after landing, the survey team discovered an abandoned <a href="/wiki/Lifeboat_(shipboard)" title="Lifeboat (shipboard)">lifeboat</a> in a small lagoon formed by the eruption<sup class="noprint selfreference">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Talk:Bouvet_Island" title="Talk:Bouvet Island">discuss</a></i>&#93;</sup>. With very little time, a brief search was made but no other signs of human activity were found, and the identity of the lifeboat remained a mystery for many years.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> On 17&#160;December 1971, the entire island and its territorial waters were protected as a <a href="/wiki/Nature_reserve" title="Nature reserve">nature reserve</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOR19711217_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOR19711217-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> A scientific landing was made in 1978, during which the underground temperature was measured to be 25&#160;°C (77&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-Rubin-2005_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rubin-2005-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> In addition to scientific surveys,<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> the lifeboat found by the <i>Protector</i> team was recovered from Nyrøysa, although no other signs of people were found.<sup id="cite_ref-Rubin-2005_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rubin-2005-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The lifeboat was believed to belong to a Soviet scientific reconnaissance vessel.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Vela_incident" title="Vela incident">Vela incident</a> took place on 22&#160;September 1979, on or above the sea between Bouvetøya and <a href="/wiki/Prince_Edward_Islands" title="Prince Edward Islands">Prince Edward Islands</a>, when the American <a href="/wiki/Vela_(satellite)" title="Vela (satellite)">Vela Hotel</a> satellite&#160;6911 registered an unexplained <a href="/wiki/Effects_of_nuclear_explosions" title="Effects of nuclear explosions">double flash</a>. This observation has been variously interpreted as a meteor, or an instrumentation glitch, but most independent assessments conclude it was an <a href="/wiki/South_Africa_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction#Nuclear_weapons" title="South Africa and weapons of mass destruction">undeclared joint nuclear test</a> carried out by South Africa and Israel.<sup id="cite_ref-Rubin-2005_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rubin-2005-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Hersh-1991_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hersh-1991-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Rhodes-2011_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rhodes-2011-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Weiss-2011_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Weiss-2011-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the mid-1980s, Bouvetøya, <a href="/wiki/Jan_Mayen" title="Jan Mayen">Jan Mayen</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Svalbard" title="Svalbard">Svalbard</a> were considered as locations for the new <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_International_Ship_Register" title="Norwegian International Ship Register">Norwegian International Ship Register</a>, but the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_convenience" title="Flag of convenience">flag of convenience</a> registry was ultimately established in <a href="/wiki/Bergen" title="Bergen">Bergen</a>, Norway, in 1987.<sup id="cite_ref-Kyvik-2008_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kyvik-2008-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 189">&#58;&#8202;189&#8202;</span></sup>
In 2007, the island was added to Norway's tentative list of nominations as a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> as part of the transnational nomination of the Mid-Atlantic Ridge.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Krill_fishery" title="Krill fishery">Krill fishing</a> in the Southern Ocean is subject to the <a href="/wiki/Convention_for_the_Conservation_of_Antarctic_Marine_Living_Resources" title="Convention for the Conservation of Antarctic Marine Living Resources">Convention for the Conservation of Antarctic Marine Living Resources</a>, which defines maximum catch quotas for a <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_fisheries" class="mw-redirect" title="Sustainable fisheries">sustainable exploitation</a> of <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_krill" title="Antarctic krill">Antarctic krill</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Schiermeier-2010_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schiermeier-2010-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Surveys conducted in 2000 showed high concentration of krill around Bouvetøya. In 2004, <a href="/wiki/Aker_BioMarine" title="Aker BioMarine">Aker BioMarine</a> was awarded a concession to fish krill, and additional quotas were awarded from 2008 for a total catch of 620,000&#160;t (610,000 long tons; 680,000 short tons).<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> There is a controversy as to whether the fisheries are sustainable, particularly in relation to krill being important food for whales.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> In 2009, Norway filed with the <a href="/wiki/UN_Commission_on_the_Limits_of_the_Continental_Shelf" class="mw-redirect" title="UN Commission on the Limits of the Continental Shelf">UN Commission on the Limits of the Continental Shelf</a> to extend the outer limit of the continental shelf past 200&#160;NM (230&#160;mi; 370&#160;km) surrounding the island.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Hanse Explorer</i> expedition ship visited Bouvet Island on 20 and 21&#160;February 2012 as part of "Expédition pour le Futur".<sup id="cite_ref-Expedition-Futur-2012-03-12_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Expedition-Futur-2012-03-12-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The expedition's goal was to land and climb the highest point on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bouvet Island is assigned the amateur radio callsign prefix 3Y0,<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> and several amateur radio <a href="/wiki/DX-pedition" title="DX-pedition">DX-peditions</a> have been conducted to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-DX-Bouvet-1989-1990_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DX-Bouvet-1989-1990-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-DX-Bouvet-3Y5X_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DX-Bouvet-3Y5X-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-DX-Bouvet-3Y0E_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DX-Bouvet-3Y0E-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/3Y0J_Bouvet_Island_DXpedition" title="3Y0J Bouvet Island DXpedition">3Y0J DX-pedition to Bouvet Island</a> took place between January and February 2023, but had to be reduced in scope and eventually cut short due to bad and worsening weather conditions.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Norvegia_Station">Norvegia Station</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Norvegia Station">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Since the 1970s, the island has been visited frequently by Norwegian Antarctic expeditions. In 1977 a temporary five-man station and an <a href="/wiki/Automated_weather_station" class="mw-redirect" title="Automated weather station">automated weather station</a> were constructed and staffed for two months in 1978 and 1979.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 64">&#58;&#8202;64&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>In March&#160;1985, a Norwegian expedition experienced sufficiently clear weather to allow the entire island to be photographed from the air, resulting in the first accurate map of the whole island, 247 years after its discovery.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> 
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Polar_Institute" title="Norwegian Polar Institute">Norwegian Polar Institute</a> established a 36&#160;m<sup>2</sup> (390&#160;sq&#160;ft) research station, made of <a href="/wiki/Shipping_container" title="Shipping container">shipping containers</a>, at Nyrøysa in 1996. On 23&#160;February 2006, the island experienced a magnitude&#160;6.2 earthquake whose epicentre was about 100&#160;km (62&#160;mi) away, <sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> weakening the station's foundation and causing it to be blown to sea during a winter storm.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In December 2012, a new research station was sent by ship from <a href="/wiki/Troms%C3%B8" title="Tromsø">Tromsø</a> in Norway, via <a href="/wiki/Cape_Town" title="Cape Town">Cape Town</a>, to Bouvet.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The robust and technically advanced station was assembled in Nyrøysa, on the north-western part of the island, the only place wide enough to land by helicopter. The elevated station is formed by three modules placed on a steel platform fixed into a concrete base. It can accommodate six people for periods of 2–4 months, and it is designed and equipped to resist rough weather conditions. The energy is supplied by wind power, which makes it easier to operate the equipment during the long periods when the station is uninhabited. The base is equipped with an automatic meteorological station that sends data via satellite throughout the year.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography_and_geology">Geography and geology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Geography and geology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg/330px-Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg/495px-Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg/660px-Bouvet_Island_topographic_map-en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="2800" data-file-height="1926" /></a><figcaption>Bouvet Island</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg/220px-Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg/330px-Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg/440px-Bouvet_Island_west_coast_glacier.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4928" data-file-height="3264" /></a><figcaption>Glacier on Bouvet Island's west coast</figcaption></figure>
<p>Bouvetøya is a volcanic island constituting the top of a <a href="/wiki/Shield_volcano" title="Shield volcano">shield volcano</a> just off the <a href="/wiki/Southwest_Indian_Ridge" title="Southwest Indian Ridge">Southwest Indian Ridge</a> in the South Atlantic Ocean.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> The island measures 9.5 by 7&#160;km (5.9 by 4.3&#160;mi) and covers an area of 49&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (19&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> including a number of small rocks and skerries and one sizable island, <a href="/wiki/Lars%C3%B8ya" title="Larsøya">Larsøya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-larsoya_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-larsoya-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It is located in the Subantarctic, south of the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_Convergence" title="Antarctic Convergence">Antarctic Convergence</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> which, by some definitions, would place the island in the <a href="/wiki/Southern_Ocean" title="Southern Ocean">Southern Ocean</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bouvet Island is one of the <a href="/wiki/Extreme_points_of_Earth" class="mw-redirect" title="Extreme points of Earth">most remote islands</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
The closest land is <a href="/wiki/Queen_Maud_Land" title="Queen Maud Land">Queen Maud Land</a> of Antarctica, which is 1,700&#160;km (1,100&#160;mi) to the south,<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 58">&#58;&#8202;58&#8202;</span></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Gough_Island" title="Gough Island">Gough Island</a>, 1,845&#160;km (1,146&#160;mi) to the north.<sup id="cite_ref-npolar_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npolar-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The closest inhabited location is <a href="/wiki/Tristan_da_Cunha" title="Tristan da Cunha">Tristan da Cunha</a> island, 2,250&#160;km (1,400&#160;mi) to the northwest.<sup id="cite_ref-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya_20-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> To its west, the <a href="/wiki/South_Sandwich_Islands" title="South Sandwich Islands">South Sandwich Islands</a> lie about 1,900&#160;km (1,200&#160;mi) away, and to its east are the <a href="/wiki/Prince_Edward_Islands" title="Prince Edward Islands">Prince Edward Islands</a>, about 2,500&#160;km (1,600&#160;mi) away.
</p><p>Nyrøysa is a 2 by 0.5&#160;km (1.2 by 0.3&#160;mi) terrace located on the north-west coast of the island. Created by a <a href="/wiki/Rock_slide" class="mw-redirect" title="Rock slide">rock slide</a> sometime between 1955 and 1957, it is the island's easiest access point.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> It is the site of the automatic weather station.<sup id="cite_ref-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> The north-west corner is the peninsula of <a href="/wiki/Kapp_Circoncision" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapp Circoncision">Kapp Circoncision</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> From there, east to <a href="/wiki/Kapp_Valdivia" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapp Valdivia">Kapp Valdivia</a>, the coast is known as Morgenstiernekysten.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/w/index.php?title=Store_Kari&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Store Kari (page does not exist)">Store Kari</a> is an islet located 1.2&#160;km (0.75&#160;mi) east of the cape.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> From Kapp Valdivia, southeast to <a href="/wiki/Kapp_Lollo" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapp Lollo">Kapp Lollo</a>, on the east side of the island, the coast is known as <a href="/w/index.php?title=Victoria_Terrasse_(Bouvet_Island)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Victoria Terrasse (Bouvet Island) (page does not exist)">Victoria Terrasse</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> From there to <a href="/wiki/Kapp_Fie" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapp Fie">Kapp Fie</a> at the southeastern corner, the coast is known as <a href="/wiki/Mowinckelkysten" title="Mowinckelkysten">Mowinckelkysten</a>. Svartstranda is a section of <a href="/wiki/Black_sand" title="Black sand">black sand</a> which runs 1.8&#160;km (1.1&#160;mi) along the section from <a href="/w/index.php?title=Kapp_Meteor&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Kapp Meteor (page does not exist)">Kapp Meteor</a>, south to Kapp Fie.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After rounding Kapp Fie, the coast along the south side is known as Vogtkysten.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The westernmost part of it is the 300&#160;m (980&#160;ft) long shore of Sjøelefantstranda.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Off <a href="/w/index.php?title=Catoodden&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Catoodden (page does not exist)">Catoodden</a>, on the south-western corner, lies <a href="/wiki/Lars%C3%B8ya" title="Larsøya">Larsøya</a>, the only island of any size off Bouvetøya.<sup id="cite_ref-larsoya_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-larsoya-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The western coast from Catoodden north to Nyrøysa, is known as Esmarchkysten. Midway up the coast lies <a href="/w/index.php?title=Norvegiaodden&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Norvegiaodden (page does not exist)">Norvegiaodden</a> (<a href="/w/index.php?title=Kapp_Norvegia&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Kapp Norvegia (page does not exist)">Kapp Norvegia</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> and 0.5&#160;km (0.31&#160;mi) off it the skerries of Bennskjæra.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ninety-three percent of the island is covered by <a href="/wiki/Glacier" title="Glacier">glaciers</a>, giving it a domed shape.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> The summit region of the island is Wilhelmplatået, slightly to the west of the island's center.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Plateau" title="Plateau">plateau</a> is 3.5&#160;km (2.2&#160;mi) across<sup id="cite_ref-SI-Volcanism-Bouvet_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SI-Volcanism-Bouvet-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> and surrounded by several peaks.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The tallest is <a href="/wiki/Olavtoppen" title="Olavtoppen">Olavtoppen</a>, 780&#160;m (2,560&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above mean sea level">above mean sea level</a> (AMSL),<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> followed by <a href="/wiki/Lykketoppen" title="Lykketoppen">Lykketoppen</a> (766&#160;m or 2,513&#160;ft AMSL)<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Mosbytoppane" title="Mosbytoppane">Mosbytoppane</a> (670&#160;m or 2,200&#160;ft AMSL).<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Below Wilhelmplatået is the main <a href="/wiki/Caldera" title="Caldera">caldera</a> responsible for creating the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The last <a href="/wiki/Eruption" class="mw-redirect" title="Eruption">eruption</a> took place 2000&#160;BCE, producing a <a href="/wiki/Lava" title="Lava">lava</a> flow at Kapp Meteor.<sup id="cite_ref-SI-Volcanism-Bouvet_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SI-Volcanism-Bouvet-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> The volcano is presumed to be in a declining state.<sup id="cite_ref-Baker-1967_15-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Baker-1967-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The temperature 30&#160;cm (12&#160;in) below the surface is 25&#160;°C (77&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>The island's total coastline is 29.6&#160;km (18.4&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Landing on the island is very difficult, as it normally experiences high seas and features a <a href="/wiki/Steep_coast" title="Steep coast">steep coast</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> During the winter, it is surrounded by <a href="/wiki/Pack_ice" class="mw-redirect" title="Pack ice">pack ice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya_20-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-placenames-npolar-Bouvetøya-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Bouvet_Triple_Junction" title="Bouvet Triple Junction">Bouvet Triple Junction</a> is located 275&#160;km (171&#160;mi) west of Bouvet Island. It is a <a href="/wiki/Triple_junction" title="Triple junction">triple junction</a> between the <a href="/wiki/South_American_Plate" title="South American Plate">South American Plate</a>, the <a href="/wiki/African_Plate" title="African Plate">African Plate</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_Plate" title="Antarctic Plate">Antarctic Plate</a>, and of the Mid-Atlantic Ridge, the Southwest Indian Ridge and the <a href="/wiki/American%E2%80%93Antarctic_Ridge" class="mw-redirect" title="American–Antarctic Ridge">American–Antarctic Ridge</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mitchell-Livermore-etal-2000_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mitchell-Livermore-etal-2000-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tnone" style="margin-left:auto;margin-right:auto;overflow:hidden;width:auto;max-width:1249px"><div class="thumbinner"><div class="noresize" style="overflow:auto"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="West coast of Bouvet Island"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg/1241px-Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg" decoding="async" width="1241" height="200" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg/1862px-Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg/2482px-Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg 2x" data-file-width="12941" data-file-height="2086" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_Island_west_coast.jpg" title="File:Bouvet Island west coast.jpg"> </a></div>West coast of Bouvet Island</div></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island is located south of the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_Convergence" title="Antarctic Convergence">Antarctic Convergence</a>, giving it a marine <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_climate" class="mw-redirect" title="Antarctic climate">Antarctic climate</a> dominated by heavy clouds and fog. It experiences a mean temperature of −1&#160;°C (30&#160;°F),<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> with January average of 1&#160;°C (34&#160;°F) and September average of −3&#160;°C (27&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-npolar_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npolar-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The monthly high mean temperatures fluctuate little through the year.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate-Zone-bouvet-island_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate-Zone-bouvet-island-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The peak temperature of 14&#160;°C (57&#160;°F) was recorded in March&#160;1980, caused by intense sun radiation. Spot temperatures as high as 20&#160;°C (68&#160;°F) have been recorded in sunny weather on rock faces.<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 59">&#58;&#8202;59&#8202;</span></sup> The island predominantly experiences a weak <a href="/wiki/West_wind" title="West wind">west wind</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-npolar_53-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npolar-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> In spite of these severe climate conditions, Bouvet Island actually is located four degrees of latitude closer to the equator than the southernmost tip of Norway, which is located at 58°N. Its latitude – by analogy to Scandinavia – is instead similar to southern <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a>.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Nature">Nature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Nature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg/220px-Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg/330px-Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg/440px-Bouvet_aerial_photo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/NASA" title="NASA">NASA</a> image of Bouvet Island from space</figcaption></figure>
<p>The harsh climate and ice-bound terrain limits non-animal life to <a href="/wiki/Fungi" class="mw-redirect" title="Fungi">fungi</a> (<a href="/wiki/Ascomycetes" class="mw-redirect" title="Ascomycetes">ascomycetes</a> including symbiotic <a href="/wiki/Lichens" class="mw-redirect" title="Lichens">lichens</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Non-vascular_plant" title="Non-vascular plant">non-vascular plants</a> (<a href="/wiki/Mosses" class="mw-redirect" title="Mosses">mosses</a> and <a href="/wiki/Liverworts" class="mw-redirect" title="Liverworts">liverworts</a>). The flora are representative for the maritime Antarctic and are <a href="/wiki/Phytogeographically" class="mw-redirect" title="Phytogeographically">phytogeographically</a> similar to those of the <a href="/wiki/South_Sandwich_Islands" title="South Sandwich Islands">South Sandwich Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Shetland_Islands" title="South Shetland Islands">South Shetland Islands</a>. Vegetation is limited because of the ice cover, although <a href="/wiki/Snow_algae" title="Snow algae">snow algae</a> are recorded. The remaining vegetation is located in snow-free areas such as <a href="/wiki/Nunatak" title="Nunatak">nunatak</a> ridges and other parts of the summit plateau, the coastal cliffs, capes and beaches. At Nyrøysa, five species of moss, six ascomycetes (including five lichens), and twenty algae have been recorded. Most snow-free areas are so steep and subject to frequent <a href="/wiki/Avalanche" title="Avalanche">avalanches</a> that only <a href="/wiki/Crustose" title="Crustose">crustose</a> lichens and algal formations are sustainable. There are six <a href="/wiki/Endemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Endemic">endemic</a> ascomycetes, three of which are lichenized.<sup id="cite_ref-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_island.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Bouvet_island.jpg/220px-Bouvet_island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Bouvet_island.jpg/330px-Bouvet_island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Bouvet_island.jpg/440px-Bouvet_island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="521" /></a><figcaption>Cape Valdivia, Bouvet Island, 2009</figcaption></figure>
<p>The island has been designated as an <a href="/wiki/Important_Bird_Area" title="Important Bird Area">Important Bird Area</a> by <a href="/wiki/BirdLife_International" title="BirdLife International">BirdLife International</a> because of its importance as a <a href="/wiki/Breeding_ground" class="mw-redirect" title="Breeding ground">breeding ground</a> for <a href="/wiki/Seabird" title="Seabird">seabirds</a>. In 1978–1979 there were an estimated 117,000 breeding <a href="/wiki/Penguins" class="mw-redirect" title="Penguins">penguins</a> on the island, consisting of <a href="/wiki/Macaroni_penguin" title="Macaroni penguin">macaroni penguin</a> and, to a lesser extent, <a href="/wiki/Chinstrap_penguin" title="Chinstrap penguin">chinstrap penguin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ad%C3%A9lie_penguin" title="Adélie penguin">Adélie penguin</a>, although these were only estimated to be 62,000 in 1989–1990. Nyrøysa is the most important <a href="/wiki/Bird_colony" title="Bird colony">colony</a> for penguins, supplemented by Posadowskybreen, Kapp Circoncision, Norvegiaodden and across from Larsøya. <a href="/wiki/Southern_fulmar" title="Southern fulmar">Southern fulmar</a> is by far the most common non-penguin bird with 100,000&#160;individuals. Other breeding seabirds consist of <a href="/wiki/Cape_petrel" title="Cape petrel">Cape petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_prion" title="Antarctic prion">Antarctic prion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wilson%27s_storm_petrel" title="Wilson&#39;s storm petrel">Wilson's storm petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Black-bellied_storm_petrel" title="Black-bellied storm petrel">black-bellied storm petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Subantarctic_skua" class="mw-redirect" title="Subantarctic skua">subantarctic skua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southern_giant_petrel" title="Southern giant petrel">southern giant petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Snow_petrel" title="Snow petrel">snow petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slender-billed_prion" title="Slender-billed prion">slender-billed prion</a> and <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_tern" title="Antarctic tern">Antarctic tern</a>. <a href="/wiki/Kelp_gull" title="Kelp gull">Kelp gull</a> is thought to have bred on the island earlier. Non-breeding birds which can be found on the island include the <a href="/wiki/King_penguin" title="King penguin">king penguin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wandering_albatross" title="Wandering albatross">wandering albatross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Black-browed_albatross" title="Black-browed albatross">black-browed albatross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Campbell_albatross" title="Campbell albatross">Campbell albatross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_yellow-nosed_albatross" title="Atlantic yellow-nosed albatross">Atlantic yellow-nosed albatross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sooty_albatross" title="Sooty albatross">sooty albatross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Light-mantled_albatross" title="Light-mantled albatross">light-mantled albatross</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northern_giant_petrel" title="Northern giant petrel">northern giant petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_petrel" title="Antarctic petrel">Antarctic petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Blue_petrel" title="Blue petrel">blue petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Soft-plumaged_petrel" title="Soft-plumaged petrel">soft-plumaged petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kerguelen_petrel" title="Kerguelen petrel">Kerguelen petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/White-headed_petrel" title="White-headed petrel">white-headed petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fairy_prion" title="Fairy prion">fairy prion</a>, <a href="/wiki/White-chinned_petrel" title="White-chinned petrel">white-chinned petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Great_shearwater" title="Great shearwater">great shearwater</a>, <a href="/wiki/Common_diving_petrel" title="Common diving petrel">common diving petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_polar_skua" title="South polar skua">south polar skua</a> and <a href="/wiki/Parasitic_jaeger" title="Parasitic jaeger">parasitic jaeger</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife_54-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The only non-bird <a href="/wiki/Vertebrate" title="Vertebrate">vertebrates</a> on the island are <a href="/wiki/Pinniped" title="Pinniped">seals</a>, specifically the <a href="/wiki/Southern_elephant_seal" title="Southern elephant seal">southern elephant seal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_fur_seal" title="Antarctic fur seal">Antarctic fur seal</a>, which breed on the island. In 1998–1999, there were 88 elephant seal pups and 13,000&#160;fur seal pups at Nyrøysa. <a href="/wiki/Southern_right_whale" title="Southern right whale">Southern right whale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Humpback_whale" title="Humpback whale">humpback whale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fin_whale" title="Fin whale">fin whale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southern_right_whale_dolphin" title="Southern right whale dolphin">southern right whale dolphin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hourglass_dolphin" title="Hourglass dolphin">hourglass dolphin</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Killer_whale" class="mw-redirect" title="Killer whale">killer whale</a> are seen in the surrounding waters.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife_54-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hyser-Bouvetøya-birdlife-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_(cropped).JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_%28cropped%29.JPG/220px-Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_%28cropped%29.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_%28cropped%29.JPG/330px-Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_%28cropped%29.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_%28cropped%29.JPG/440px-Bouvet_Island_ISS017-E-16161_no_text_%28cropped%29.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2041" /></a><figcaption>Image courtesy of the Image Science &amp; Analysis Laboratory, NASA Johnson Space Center</figcaption></figure>
<p>Bouvetøya is one of three dependencies of Norway.<sup id="cite_ref-Gisle-1999_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gisle-1999-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Unlike <a href="/wiki/Peter_I_Island" title="Peter I Island">Peter I Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Queen_Maud_Land" title="Queen Maud Land">Queen Maud Land</a>, which are subject to the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_Treaty_System" title="Antarctic Treaty System">Antarctic Treaty System</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Barr-1987_8-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barr-1987-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page: 65">&#58;&#8202;65&#8202;</span></sup> Bouvetøya is not disputed.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_67-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The dependency status entails that the island is not part of the Kingdom of Norway, but is still under Norwegian <a href="/wiki/Sovereignty" title="Sovereignty">sovereignty</a>. This implies that the island can be <a href="/wiki/Cede" class="mw-redirect" title="Cede">ceded</a> without violating the first article of the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Norway" title="Constitution of Norway">Constitution of Norway</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Gisle-1999_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gisle-1999-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Norwegian administration of the island is handled by the Polar Affairs Department of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_and_the_Police_(Norway)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministry of Justice and the Police (Norway)">Ministry of Justice and the Police</a>, located in <a href="/wiki/Oslo" title="Oslo">Oslo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The annexation of the island is regulated by the Dependency Act of 24&#160;March 1933. It establishes that Norwegian <a href="/wiki/Criminal_law" title="Criminal law">criminal law</a>, <a href="/wiki/Private_law" title="Private law">private law</a> and <a href="/wiki/Procedural_law" title="Procedural law">procedural law</a> apply to the island, in addition to other laws that explicitly state they are valid on the island. It further establishes that all land belongs to the state, and prohibits the storage and detonation of nuclear products.<sup id="cite_ref-LOV19300227_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LOV19300227-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bouvet Island has been designated with the <a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2" title="ISO 3166-2">ISO 3166-2</a> code <a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:BV" title="ISO 3166-2:BV">BV</a><sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> and was subsequently awarded the <a href="/wiki/Country_code" title="Country code">country code</a> <a href="/wiki/Top-level_domain" title="Top-level domain">top-level domain</a> <a href="/wiki/.bv" title=".bv">.bv</a> on 21&#160;August 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The domain is managed by <a href="/wiki/Norid" title="Norid">Norid</a> but is not in use.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> surrounding the island covers an area of 441,163&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (170,334&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/King_penguin" title="King penguin">king penguin</a> in <a href="/wiki/Edinburgh_Zoo" title="Edinburgh Zoo">Edinburgh Zoo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nils_Olav" title="Nils Olav">Major General Sir Nils Olav III</a>, carries the title <i>Baron of the Bouvet Islands</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_fiction">In fiction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: In fiction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>The island figures prominently in the book <i>A Grue of Ice</i> (1962), an adventure novel by <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_Jenkins" title="Geoffrey Jenkins">Geoffrey Jenkins</a>, based on <a href="/wiki/Tristan_da_Cunha" title="Tristan da Cunha">Tristan da Cunha</a>, Bouvet, and the mythical <a href="/wiki/Thompson_Island_(South_Atlantic)" title="Thompson Island (South Atlantic)">Thompson Island</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Jenkins-1962_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jenkins-1962-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Bouvet is the setting of the 2004 film <i><a href="/wiki/Alien_vs._Predator_(film)" title="Alien vs. Predator (film)">Alien vs. Predator</a></i>, which uses its Norwegian name "Bouvetøya".<sup id="cite_ref-Alien-Predator-2004_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Alien-Predator-2004-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Explanatory_notes">Explanatory notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Bouvet_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Explanatory notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-3">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The internet domain suffix .bv is allocated to Bouvetøya, but has never been used.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-26"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-26">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The scientific reconnaissance vessel 'Slava-9' began its regular 13th cruise with the 'Slava' Antarctic whaling fleet on 22&#160;October 1958 ... On 27&#160;November she got to Bouvet Island. A group of sailors landed, but were unable to leave the island in time because of worsened weather and stayed on it for about 3&#160;days. The people were withdrawn by helicopter on 29&#160;November 1958.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-36"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-36">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The first four climbers (Aaron Halstead, Will Allen, Bruno Rodi and Jason Rodi) were the first humans to climb the highest peak. A <a href="/wiki/Time_capsule" title="Time capsule">time capsule</a> containing the top visions of the future for 2062 was left behind. The next morning, Aaron Halstead led five other climbers (Sarto Blouin, Seth Sherman, Chakib Bouayed, <a href="/wiki/Cindy_Sampson" title="Cindy Sampson">Cindy Sampson</a>, and Akos Hivekovics) to the top.<sup id="cite_ref-Expedition-Futur-2012-03-12_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Expedition-Futur-2012-03-12-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
